## 7. The exact Lovable design prompt we used (for reference / reuse)

> Build a polished, modern paper trading platform called **PaperTrader** — a web app where users practice investing with virtual money and run "what if I had invested" simulations. No real money. Build the full UI with realistic mock data; backend + market-data API connected later.
>
> Design system: clean fintech aesthetic (Robinhood meets Bloomberg-lite), dark mode default + light toggle, green gains / red losses, shadcn/ui + Recharts, Inter font, responsive.
>
> Screens: (1) Landing, (2) Auth login/signup, (3) Dashboard with stat cards + portfolio value chart with 1D/1W/1M/3M/1Y/ALL toggles + holdings table + watchlist/top-movers sidebar, (4) Stock detail with price chart + key stats + buy/sell panel (market & limit) + tabs, (5) What-If Simulator (stock + date + amount → value today, charted vs S&P 500 — make it impressive), (6) Markets searchable list with sparklines, (7) Portfolio/Activity with allocation donut + transaction history, (8) Settings with theme toggle + reset to $100k.
>
> Global: left sidebar nav, top bar with global search + avatar. Every user starts with $100,000 virtual cash. Mock data using AAPL/TSLA/NVDA/MSFT/AMZN/GOOGL with realistic histories. Structure so mock data is easy to swap for real API calls. Prioritize beautiful, cohesive, production-quality design; charts and What-If simulator are the visual highlights.

## 8. Changelog

- **2026-08-14 (`verify-hardening-pass.ts` flake fixed at the root — BUILT, VERIFIED with 3 consecutive full-suite runs, committed locally) — NOT pushed.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** the script had now failed twice on a ~$2 live-price mismatch and passed on isolated re-run both times — an intermittently-red test is worse than a consistently-red one, since it teaches you to ignore failures, which is how a real regression eventually slips through. Root-cause it and fix at the right layer: candidate (a) is the test fetching the price twice and comparing when it should capture once and reuse; candidate (b) is an exact assertion that should tolerate a documented small drift. Prefer (a) if the double-fetch is genuinely unnecessary. Do not loosen the assertion beyond what real live-data variance justifies, and comment WHY. Then run the full suite three times and confirm 30/30 on all three — one green run doesn't prove a flake is fixed.
  - **Root cause, found by reading both sides of the comparison, not guessed:** the 4-way valuation reconciliation block (§2 of the script) builds its own "Dashboard total" by fetching each held symbol's price via `getServerQuote()` — which reads through `cache.server.ts`'s `TTL.quote = 30_000` (a 30-second cache, entirely appropriate for a normal page view). It then compares that number against `getPositionsValue()`'s return, and separately against `runSnapshots()`'s own total — both of which are REAL production functions that DELIBERATELY never use the quote cache for a money-critical number (`lib/margin/valuation.server.ts`'s own doc comment: *"Never a cached/stale number for a money decision: stock holdings and option positions are both re-priced live at call time"* — a correct, deliberate design choice, not something to weaken). Comparing a value that could be up to 30 real seconds stale against an always-fresh one WILL occasionally show a few dollars of drift on an actively-trading symbol — not a bug in either code path, just an avoidable mismatch in how the TEST sourced its own reference number.
  - **Fixed via candidate (a), confirmed genuinely unnecessary and eliminated, not just shrunk-and-tolerated:** the test's holdings-value loop (one `getServerQuote()` — cached — call per holding, sequentially) was replaced with ONE batched, uncached `providerQuotes(symbols)` call — the EXACT SAME mechanism `getPositionsValue()` uses internally. This removes the dominant, structural source of drift (up to 30s of cache staleness) entirely; what remains is only the real, unavoidable network gap between two genuinely independent live fetches to two separate production functions (`getPositionsValue()` and `runSnapshots()`, each correctly designed to fetch fresh every call — neither can be made to share a single fetch without either mocking one of them, which would defeat the point of a zero-mocks, real-Supabase-and-real-Finnhub integration test, or modifying a money-critical production function's signature just for testability, a change with a much larger blast radius than this flake warrants).
  - **The residual, unavoidable gap gets a small, explicitly-justified tolerance** (candidate (b), applied only to the genuinely-irreducible remainder): a new `closeToLive(a, b)` helper — `max(2¢, 0.05% of the reference value)` — applied to the 3 assertions that legitimately span two independent live-fetch call sites. Calibrated, not guessed: the original bug this test exists to catch (Dashboard/snapshot showing GROSS assets instead of NET equity) overstated portfolio value by the FULL margin loan amount — in the real incident that was found, $61,062.66, roughly four orders of magnitude larger than this tolerance. A tolerance tight enough to still catch that class of bug, loose enough to absorb realistic sub-second market-tick noise on a liquid symbol. The two assertions with NO live-fetch race (pure arithmetic on already-captured numbers, `oldBuggyTotal - dashboardTotal === marginLoan` and the option-expiry loan-paydown check) were deliberately left on the original exact 2¢ `closeTo()` — loosening those would have been unjustified, since nothing about them touches a second independent fetch.
  - **Verified properly, not with one lucky run, per explicit instruction:** ran `verify-hardening-pass.ts` twice in isolation first (both clean, reconciliation numbers matching exactly to the cent — $22400.74 vs $22400.74, $15000 vs $15000 — the tolerance never actually had to absorb anything), then the FULL 30-script `verify-*.ts` suite **three consecutive times: 30/30 green on all three runs, zero `❌` markers in any of the 90 script executions**, and specifically confirmed the fixed reconciliation assertions passed with EXACT matches in all three (not just "within tolerance" — genuinely zero drift observed across 3 independent real runs). `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean.
  - **Commit:** `verify-hardening-pass.ts` (the fetch fix + `closeToLive` + comments) + this HANDOFF update, one commit — a single-file, single-purpose fix with no natural split point. **NOT pushed — reporting first per standing convention.**

- **2026-08-14 (Post-audit Tier-2 fix pass — 6 items, BUILT, VERIFIED with real evidence, committed locally in chunks) — NOT pushed. Issues #20–#25 filed and In progress.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** fix the dead `#simulator` nav anchor first; instrument the ~8 uninstrumented features (item 9) as the prerequisite for answering why scenario challenges have zero runs; group the sidebar (item 6); add a zero-new-API-cost news/insight row indicator (item 7); add one discreet scenario-challenges entry point; build a proactive Coach nudge gated on the Coach's own real statistical thresholds (item 10), never a separate trade-count rule. Verify each with real evidence, 375px + desktop, `track()` events confirmed landing in `analytics_events`, full suite 30/30 green with no footnotes.
  - **1. Dead nav link.** `index.tsx`'s "Simulator" nav item was `<a href="#simulator">` — no matching `id` existed anywhere on the page, a complete dead click for every visitor. Fixed to a real `Link to="/simulator"`. Also verified (not assumed) that `#features`/`#how` resolve to real elements and genuinely scroll on click — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` + real click-through, both landed at the correct `scrollY`.
  - **2. Instrumented all ~8 previously-dark features.** New events: `insight_generated` (fires ONLY on a genuine cache-miss/fresh-Claude-call in `insights.server.ts` — deliberately distinct from the pre-existing `insight_viewed`, which fires on every view including cache hits, so the two together finally separate "real generation cost" from "page views"), `scenario_started`/`scenario_completed` (`scenarios/functions.ts`, the completion event fires only the FIRST time a run's `final_score` is set, never on a later re-read of an already-completed run), `margin_enabled` (`margin/functions.ts`, only on the `enabled:true` branch), `feature_unlocked` (`coaching/functions.ts`'s `unlockFeatureFn`, parameterized by `feature` so one event covers both options and margin unlocks rather than two near-duplicate names), and 3 client-only events with no existing server function to hang off (`watchlist_add`, `journal_entry_created`, `coach_visited`) — new `lib/analytics/functions.ts`/`api.ts`, a closed-enum JWT-verified passthrough specifically scoped to those 3, not a general-purpose event sink. `journal_entry_created` carries `symbol` + a `linkedToTrade` boolean, deliberately never `body`/`title` — the same privacy discipline `journal_entries` has had since step 6.
    - **Real bug found and fixed during live verification:** `coach_visited` fired TWICE within ~1.5ms of a single real page visit (caught via a direct `analytics_events` query, not assumed). Root cause not conclusively identified (no `<StrictMode>` anywhere in app or framework code was found on inspection — this isn't the usual React 18 dev-double-invoke explanation), but the ~1.5ms gap is consistent with the same effect body executing twice within one synchronous flush rather than a true unmount/remount. - **2026-08-12/13 (PLAN.md §6 step 10 — B4 super-admin console — BUILT, VERIFIED live end-to-end incl. a real non-admin JWT calling every admin server function directly, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Issue #10 moved to "In progress" on the board.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** admin identity as the single most security-sensitive part (writable only server-side, no client path, first admin set manually via SQL, self-granting structurally impossible); user list/search/suspend/delete (reusing the verified delete cascade); a usage+cost dashboard with HONEST estimates (assumed rate × real count, labeled as an estimate); system health; an immutable audit log ("an admin console without an audit trail is a liability"); and a HARD CONSTRAINT — `journal_entries` gets NO `service_role` grant from this feature, and NO user impersonation (the single most dangerous admin-console feature, would specifically defeat the journal privacy boundary), documented as a deliberate non-goal rather than an oversight.
  - **Migration `0026_admin_console.sql`** — `profiles.is_admin`/`suspended_at` + `admin_audit_log` (RLS on, zero policies, `grant select, insert` to `service_role` only — NO update/delete grant exists anywhere, ever). `admin_audit_log.admin_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (not cascade, not set null) — **an admin cannot make their own audit history disappear by deleting their account**, a deliberate design choice that came back into play during cleanup (see below). Two SECURITY DEFINER RPCs, both re-verifying `is_admin` INSIDE the function (defense in depth beyond the TS-layer check, matching this project's standing "hiding a nav item is never the security boundary" rule): `admin_set_suspended` (atomic flag-update + audit row in one transaction, self-suspend guarded) and `admin_log_action` (generic audit writer for view/delete flows). `is_admin` has genuinely NO write path anywhere — not the client, not `service_role` — only a superuser SQL session; **deliberately no promotion RPC was built at all**, since removing the feature is a stronger guarantee than trying to secure one.
  - **`lib/admin/functions.ts`** — 7 JWT-verified server functions (`listUsersFn`/`getUserDetailFn`/`setUserSuspendedFn`/`deleteUserFn`/`getUsageStatsFn`/`getSystemHealthFn`/`getAuditLogFn`), every one calling `requireAdmin(userId)` (a fresh, uncached `is_admin` read on every call) before any privileged work. `getUserDetailFn` composes profile + auth + holdings + transactions + option positions/transactions + agent config/holdings + scenario runs — explicitly never touches `journal_entries`. `setUserSuspendedFn` calls the atomic RPC first, then the GoTrue ban second (documented ordering: a flag-set-but-ban-failed state is visible and safe; the reverse would be a silent audit gap). `deleteUserFn` reuses the exact same `admin.auth.admin.deleteUser()` + FK-cascade mechanism as self-service delete-account — logs 'delete_user' BEFORE the delete (target row still exists, FK-satisfiable) and 'delete_user_completed'/'failed' AFTER with `target_user_id: null` (the row is gone by then) and the email preserved in `detail` instead. **Cost estimates are honest by construction**: `ESTIMATED_COST_PER_INSIGHT_CALL_USD`/`ESTIMATED_COST_PER_AGENT_RUN_USD` are documented flat-rate assumptions (no real token-usage capture exists anywhere in this codebase — confirmed by reading both Anthropic call sites, neither reads `res.usage`), and the UI always shows the assumed rate next to the total rather than presenting it as measured. Provider-fetch count (an exact `price_cache.fetched_at` count in the window) is shown as a REAL cache-miss signal, deliberately not dressed up as a fabricated hit-rate percentage since true hit/miss isn't instrumented (would require a write on the hottest read path).
  - **🐛 Real bug found + fixed during verification:** `getUserDetailFn`'s `view_user` audit-log write was fire-and-forget (`void admin.rpc(...)`) and empirically confirmed to silently drop the row — a direct DB query after a real page view showed zero matching audit entries. Fixed by awaiting it and surfacing an error if the log write itself fails, prioritizing audit completeness (a hard kickoff requirement) over the ~150ms latency cost; re-verified via both a direct DB query and the live Audit Log UI showing the entry.
  - **UI**: `/app/admin` (client-side redirect for non-admins — explicitly documented as UX only, not the security boundary) with Overview/Users/Audit Log tabs. Overview: system health rows + honest cost/usage cards + a disclosure banner + top-20 per-user cost outliers. Users: search-by-email list → detail page with Suspend/Unsuspend + Delete (both behind `ConfirmDialog`, delete using `requireTypedConfirmation="DELETE"` matching the self-service pattern) and 4 cards — the Journal card shows ONLY a static "private, not visible by design" message and never queries `journal_entries`. Audit Log: immutable, human-readable action labels, with a `detail.email` fallback for post-delete rows whose `target_email` FK went to null. Admin-only nav entry (sidebar + mobile) is explicitly commented as UX convenience, matching the same non-boundary framing as the route redirect.
  - **🚨 A THIRD multi-hour silent hang during this step (~4h24m, killed by explicit CTO order) — full incident writeup already in the dedicated 🚨 section near the top of this file.** Root cause: `getServiceClient()` had zero fetch timeout, the exact gap the 2026-08-11 incident had identified and explicitly deferred. Fixed at the source (both the server admin client and the browser client now carry a hard `AbortController` timeout on every Supabase call) plus a new standing rule — no throwaway scripts, ever; everything routes through `verify-harness.ts` from the first line.
  - **Live verification (`verify-admin-live.ts`, real Supabase, zero mocks, 40/40 checks) proved:** `is_admin` unwritable from an authenticated client (42501 permission denied) and no RPC exists to grant it; a non-admin `p_admin_id` independently rejected by both SQL functions via direct tampered RPC calls (bypassing the TS layer entirely); journal privacy proven three ways — build-time grep (zero matches outside comments), a live rejected `service_role` SELECT on `journal_entries` (no grant exists), and a real journal secret string confirmed absent from the admin detail-view payload; suspend genuinely blocks a real `signInWithPassword` ("User is banned") and unsuspend restores it; self-suspend guard proven safely against the real admin account (the RPC's guard fires before any write); delete leaves zero rows across every dependent table; cost numbers reconcile exactly (`count × documented rate constant`) via a before/after delta on freshly-created throwaway users, avoiding contamination from real background activity.
  - **Then re-verified live in the browser, per explicit follow-up instruction** — all 4 admin pages (Overview, Users, User detail, Audit Log) confirmed rendering correctly at both desktop and 375px mobile. **The headline security proof**: signed in as a freshly-created throwaway NON-admin, then in the real browser (not a script) dynamic-imported `lib/admin/api.ts` and called all 7 admin client-wrapper functions DIRECTLY with that user's real, valid JWT — bypassing the UI and the route entirely, hitting the actual `/_serverFn/...` HTTP endpoint through the framework's own client-RPC machinery. **All 7 rejected**, every one with "You don't have access to the admin console." Confirmed the route-level redirect separately (secondary, UX-level check) — the same non-admin navigating to `/app/admin` was client-side redirected to the Dashboard.
  - **Cleanup constraint discovered, not routed around:** the throwaway admin account used for browser verification could NOT be hard-deleted — `admin_audit_log.admin_id`'s `ON DELETE RESTRICT` (see migration notes above) is doing exactly its documented job, since this account had real audit rows from the verification itself. Deleting those rows to unblock the account delete would have violated the explicit "audit log is immutable, no delete grant, ever" hard constraint from the kickoff, so that workaround was rejected. Instead, the account was **permanently banned** (`ban_duration: "876000h"`, ~100 years — blocks sign-in AND token refresh at the GoTrue layer, the same mechanism proven above for suspend), which fully satisfies the actual security goal (no admin-capable, sign-in-able account lingers) while leaving the row itself for Venky to decide on. The two other throwaway accounts (browser-target, non-admin) had no audit rows referencing them and were cleanly hard-deleted, confirmed via `getUserById` returning no user for all three. Zero real accounts (`santosh.naranapatty@`, `santoshthegamer@`, `pcvenky10@`, `rajath.anil@`, `venkiel12@`, `venkatpraveen1@`) were touched at any point — enforced structurally by an email-pattern allowlist gate before every destructive call, not just by intent. **RESOLVED 2026-08-13, see the follow-up entry directly below** — the underlying `ON DELETE RESTRICT` design was corrected (migration `0027`), and this account is now genuinely deleted, not merely banned.

  - **Migration `0027_admin_audit_log_set_null.sql`**: drops the old FK (found by introspecting `information_schema` rather than a hardcoded constraint name — Postgres's auto-generated name is a convention, not a guarantee), drops `admin_id`'s `not null`, re-adds the FK as `on delete set null`. A defensive backfill (`update ... where admin_email is null`) is included as a real safety net even though it's expected to affect 0 rows — `admin_email` has been `not null` and populated at write time since day one. **The corrected invariant, stated precisely: the audit HISTORY is immutable, not the account row** — a `🔄 SUPERSEDED` annotation was added to 0026's own original comment (comment-only, the applied SQL there is untouched, so the historical record of what actually ran stays accurate) pointing here for the real reasoning.
  - `AdminAuditLog.admin_id` narrowed from `string` to `string | null` in `supabase/types.ts`; confirmed the UI never needed a change since `app.admin.audit.tsx` already displays `admin_email`, never `admin_id`.

  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** "Trade the 2008 crash" — drop the user into a real historical period with a fixed stake, step through it day-by-day/week-by-week trading at real historical prices WITHOUT seeing future prices, score at the end against buy-and-hold and best/worst single-stock outcomes. Scope v1: 3 curated scenarios, stocks only (no options/margin), week-stepping. **THE CRITICAL CORRECTNESS PROPERTY is no-look-ahead** — structurally enforced server-side, verified by inspecting actual network payloads, not just script assertions. Isolation from the real paper portfolio (own tables, own cash, mirroring the AI agent's sub-portfolio pattern). Unlocked for now (payment gating deferred to step 11, structured to slot in later without touching the scenario engine).
  - **Migration `0025_scenario_challenges.sql`** — `scenario_runs` (status active/completed, cash, starting_cash, `step_index`, `final_score` jsonb, partial unique index enforcing one active run per scenario per user) / `scenario_holdings` / `scenario_transactions` (append-only). Four SECURITY DEFINER functions, all revoked from public/anon/authenticated and granted execute to `service_role` only: `start_scenario_run`, `advance_scenario_step`, `execute_scenario_trade`, `finalize_scenario_run` — the exact same lockdown shape as every other money-moving function in this schema (`execute_trade`, `agent_execute_trade`, `set_margin_enabled`).
  - **`lib/scenarios/catalog.ts`** — code-defined (not DB rows) catalog of 3 scenarios: 2008 financial crisis (Jun 2008–Jun 2009, AIG/C/BAC/AAPL/WMT/XOM), 2020 COVID crash (Jan–Aug 2020, ZM/AMZN/CCL/DAL/TSLA/XOM), 2022 bear market (Jan–Dec 2022, NFLX/META/TSLA/AAPL/WMT/XOM), each $10,000 starting stake, SPY benchmark, 5-trading-day step size, and a historically-accurate debrief string shown on completion. Symbol/date choices were verified against REAL Twelve Data availability (`verify-scenario-data-availability.ts`) before being finalized, not assumed.
  - **No-look-ahead, the property this feature lives or dies on:** `scenario_runs.step_index` is the ONLY source of truth for how far a run has progressed, written ONLY by `advance_scenario_step` (the RPC only ever adds a small fixed `p_steps` increment once per call — even a maliciously large client-supplied `p_max_index` can't cause a multi-step jump). The benchmark symbol's own candle series IS the scenario's trading-day calendar; `lib/scenarios/calendar.ts`'s `sliceUpToDate` is the actual boundary, called inside `getScenarioMarketDataFn` — the ONE code path that ever returns candle data to the browser for an active run. A completed run legitimately reveals the full window (cutoff = scenario end date) for post-hoc review, since there's no lookahead concern once it's over.
  - **`lib/scenarios/scoring.ts`** (pure) — `computeScenarioScore` takes final cash/holdings + start/end prices for every scenario symbol, returns user return %, benchmark (buy-and-hold SPY) return %, best/worst single-stock outcome, and beat-benchmark flag. `lib/scenarios/functions.ts` — `startScenarioRunFn`/`getScenarioMarketDataFn`/`advanceScenarioStepFn`/`executeScenarioTradeFn`, same JWT-verified service-role pattern as every other trading feature; `advanceScenarioStepFn` auto-finalizes and scores the run the moment `step_index` reaches the real max.
  - **UI**: `/app/scenarios` picker (3 scenario cards, Start/Resume/View-results, deliberately NOT wrapped in `UnlockGate` — that's step 8's comprehension gate, not a payment gate) and `/app/scenarios/$runId` (simulated-date + step progress, Advance button, trade panel, holdings, trade history, and — once completed — a full results block: return vs. benchmark, best/worst single stock, per-symbol outcome table, debrief). No `ConfirmDialog` on scenario trades (deliberate, unlike every real-money-adjacent trade elsewhere): this is sandbox cash inside an already-isolated scenario, rapid back-and-forth trading through 30–50 steps is the whole point.
  - 🐛 **A real routing bug was found and fixed during browser verification, not left for a real user to hit:** TanStack Router's file-based routing infers parent-child nesting from filename prefix — since `app.scenarios.$runId.tsx` shares the `app.scenarios` prefix with the picker's own `app.scenarios.tsx`, the router silently nested the run-detail route AS A CHILD of the picker route. The picker page had no `<Outlet/>`, so navigating to a run rendered the URL/page-title correctly but the DOM stayed on the picker's own JSX — invisible to a script-only check, only caught by actually looking at the rendered page in the browser. Fixed the standard TanStack Router way: split the picker into a thin `app.scenarios.tsx` layout (`component: () => <Outlet/>`) plus `app.scenarios.index.tsx` carrying the picker's actual content, so both the picker and `$runId` are proper siblings under the layout. Confirmed via the regenerated `routeTree.gen.ts` and a fresh browser click-through.
  - **Live verification (`verify-scenarios-live.ts`, real Supabase + real Twelve Data, zero mocks) proved, among 37 checks:** a full 33-step real playthrough of 2020-covid with ZERO look-ahead violations at every single step; a fabricated `stepIndexOverride`/`cutoffDate`/`futureDate` field in the market-data call has ZERO effect (there's no such input field to smuggle a value through); a direct RPC tamper with a maliciously huge `p_max_index` still only advances `step_index` by the real `p_steps` (5), never further; heavy scenario trading left the REAL account's `cash_balance`/margin state/holdings/transactions row counts byte-identical before/after while the scenario's own holdings correctly picked up the trades; 3 real scenario transaction prices matched independently-refetched raw Twelve Data closes EXACTLY; a full 2022-bear playthrough (50 steps) reconciled finalCash/finalHoldingsValue/finalPortfolioValue/userReturnPct/benchmarkReturnPct/bestSingleStock/worstSingleStock to the cent against an independent hand-computation; a completed run re-read from a fresh query is idempotent (`deepEqual` on `final_score`, not `JSON.stringify` — see incident below) and correctly reveals the full window; starting a second run of an already-active scenario is rejected; RLS proven between two real users (can't read each other's runs, own run list correctly excludes the other's). **Then re-verified live in the actual browser**, including inspecting the raw network response body of `getScenarioMarketDataFn`: step 1 payload contains exactly 1 candle per symbol dated the scenario's own start date; after one "Advance" click the payload contains exactly 6 candles per symbol, none past the new cutoff date; a completed run's payload legitimately spans the full 249-candle window. A real trade (1 ZM @ $72.62) executed correctly and the Dashboard for the SAME logged-in user, checked immediately after, still read $25,000.00 / 0 holdings — isolation confirmed in the browser, not just the DB. 375px + desktop screenshots captured for the picker, an active run mid-playthrough, and a completed run's results page.
  - **Two incidents hit mid-build, both fixed structurally (see the two dedicated 🚨 sections above this changelog for full narrative) — not just patched for this one script:** (1) an ad hoc debug script that skipped every mandated hardened-harness protection hung SILENTLY for ~2 hours; root-caused to a complete absence of fetch timeouts anywhere in the market-data/Supabase-client stack, fixed via a `fetchWithTimeout()` (20s `AbortController`) added to `provider.server.ts`'s two raw `fetch()` call sites, and — the real structural fix — a new shared `verify-harness.ts` (`step`/`withTimeout`/`assert`/`deepEqual`/`sleep`/`createTestUser`/`runVerification`) that every verify script (this step's and all 14 pre-existing ones) now routes through, with `runVerification`'s global-ceiling timeout as the backstop for a script that forgets one particular step's own wrap. (2) A second, compounding incident on the SAME debug script: `admin.auth.admin.createUser()` called without `user_metadata.terms_accepted_version` hit migration `0022`'s `handle_new_user()` trigger, which surfaced as an opaque Postgres/GoTrue 500 — misdiagnosed at first as a Supabase platform outage before the Auth error logs made the real cause legible. Fixed with `createTestUser()` in the shared harness (always attaches the required metadata) and every one of the ~20 existing direct-`createUser` call sites retrofitted to use it — confirmed via `grep` that zero direct calls remain outside the harness itself.
  - **Full regression suite (28 `verify-*.ts` scripts) run clean** — 27 real passes, `verify-margin-ui.ts` correctly excluded (it's an argv-driven manual ops CLI, not a self-checking test, same as noted in step 8's entry). One transient step-level timeout hit `verify-hardening-pass.ts` on a full end-to-end 28-script run (cumulative API/DB load after ~20 preceding live-verify scripts); re-ran it in isolation immediately after and it passed clean end-to-end — not a regression from this step's changes. typecheck clean, production build clean, secret grep clean.

- **2026-08-11 (PLAN.md §6 step 8 — B3 adaptive coaching + progressive unlocks — BUILT, VERIFIED live end-to-end incl. real browser UI, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Issue #8 moved to "In progress" on the board.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** teach to where the user actually is. Two halves — surfacing the right lesson at the right time (built on step 7's signals), and gating options/margin behind UNDERSTANDING, never behind performance. **HARD PRINCIPLE, stated in the migration's own header comment and re-derived structurally below:** unlocks are earned by demonstrating comprehension, never by winning — the exact reason PLAN.md §1 rejected "increase risk for successful users." No code anywhere in this step reads `cash_balance`, a return, or any P&L figure as an input to an unlock or a level.
  - **Migration `0024_progressive_unlocks.sql`** — `profiles.options_unlocked_at`/`margin_unlocked_at` (nullable `timestamptz`, matching `terms_accepted_at`'s own precedent: null=locked, non-null=the moment they passed). `unlock_feature(p_user_id, p_feature)` is the ONLY write path — mirrors `set_margin_enabled`'s exact `SECURITY DEFINER` shape (`select...for update` → `if not found raise profile_not_found` → idempotent early-return on an already-set timestamp → single `update` → `revoke all / grant execute to service_role`). GRANDFATHERING is deliberately NOT a backfill `UPDATE`: it's computed at READ time as `unlocked_at IS NOT NULL OR real historical activity exists`, so it's self-healing and correct for any account regardless of when it was created relative to this migration. Deliberately untouched by `reset_paper_account`: resetting wipes trading STATE, not already-demonstrated comprehension.
  - **`lib/coaching/` — four pure modules, unit-tested first (34/34 synthetic assertions), server/client wiring after:**
    - `quiz.ts` — isomorphic (imported by both client render and server validation) question bank, 3 questions each for options/margin targeting the concepts that actually hurt people (the 100x multiplier, expiring worthless, leverage amplifying losses, what a margin call does), each with re-teach copy. Correct answers are deliberately visible in the client bundle — documented in the file's own header as fine, since this is an educational gate on a simulator, not a security boundary; the server still independently re-validates every submission.
    - `unlocks.ts` — `computeUnlockStatus(unlockedAt, hasPriorActivity)` → `{unlocked, reason: "quiz"|"grandfathered"|"locked", unlockedAt}`. A recorded timestamp always takes precedence over grandfathering when both are true.
    - `priority.ts` — `pickTopLesson(analytics)` picks AT MOST ONE lesson from step 7's `BehavioralAnalytics`, respecting its `available` gate (never surfaces from an unreliable sample) AND requiring the metric's own boolean signature (silence is the common, correct case for a healthy account). Priority order: disposition, revenge-trading, over-trading, concentration, win-rate, journal-correlation (last because it's positive reinforcement, not a warning).
    - `level.ts` — `computeExperienceLevel(inputs)`: new/developing/experienced from 4 fixed, named, transparent criteria (trades placed, distinct instruments traded, journal entries written, distinct symbols currently held) — a level requires clearing ≥3 of 4 bars, so no single missing habit permanently locks someone out. Returns the full breakdown (`criteria[]`, `nextLevelNeeds[]`) so the UI shows its work, never a hidden score. `ExperienceInputs` structurally has no price/return/balance field.
  - **`lib/coaching/functions.ts` (`unlockFeatureFn`) + `queries.ts`/`api.ts`** — same JWT-verified service-role pattern as `lib/margin/functions.ts`. Reads (`getRawUnlockInputs`/`getExperienceInputs`) are plain RLS-scoped client queries (no server function needed) — `option_transactions`/`margin_events`/`transactions`/`holdings` are all already `SELECT`-granted to `authenticated`, and `journal_entries` is read ONLY for a COUNT via the user's own session, never content, same discipline step 7 established for that table's deliberate lack of a `service_role` grant. The one WRITE path re-runs `checkAnswers()` server-side against the canonical bank before ever calling `unlock_feature()` — a scripted client can't self-grant by sending a fabricated "all correct" flag.
  - **`components/coaching/UnlockGate.tsx`** — wraps the gated content: unlocked → renders children; locked → an inviting card ("Options are powerful and easy to misuse — a 2-minute primer, then they're yours") that opens a dialog with a short primer, then a 3-question quiz graded INSTANTLY client-side per question (comparing against `quiz.ts`'s own data — same "not a security boundary" reasoning) so a wrong answer re-teaches that exact point and allows immediate retry, no waiting, no penalty; the final submission still round-trips through the real server RPC as the authority. Wired into `/app/options`, the stock-detail Options tab, and `/app/margin`.
  - **`/app/coach` extended** (step 7's page) with an Experience Level card (badge + per-criterion progress bars + "to advance" copy + an explicit "never on whether you're up or down" line) and a "Right now, this is worth your attention" hero that shows `pickTopLesson`'s pick using the SAME copy-builder functions as the full breakdown below it (no duplicated copy) — or a quiet "nothing stands out" line when nothing's triggered.
  - **Live verification (`verify-coaching-live.ts`, real Supabase, 8 throwaway users, 40+ checks, zero mocks) proved, among other things:** a fresh account sees both features honestly locked; a wrong quiz answer never calls `unlock_feature` (profile checked via admin, still null) and an immediate retry with corrected answers succeeds; the unlock persists across a simulated session refresh (brand-new client, fresh sign-in) and a 2nd `unlock_feature` call is idempotent (returns the ORIGINAL timestamp); **grandfathering proven explicitly both ways** — a user with one real `option_transactions` row, and a user who enabled-then-disabled margin, both read `unlocked=true, reason=grandfathered` with `unlocked_at` NEVER written, and each stays locked on the OTHER feature (per-feature, not global); `reset_paper_account` leaves `margin_unlocked_at` byte-identical while `cash_balance` resets to $25,000; a planted revenge-trading signature (5 losses, each followed by 3× oversized re-entries — deliberately size-based, not time-based, since real trades in a script happen in seconds) makes `pickTopLesson` return exactly `"revengeTrading"` with disposition/over-trading honestly unavailable, while a sparse account returns `null`; **the thesis test** — a diligent-but-losing account (6 symbols, 3 journal entries, 2 open positions, every closed trade a real -2% loss, P&L **−$80.00**) ranked `"developing"`, strictly ABOVE a careless-but-winning account (1 symbol, 0 journal entries, every closed trade a real +2% gain, P&L **+$180.00**) which stayed `"new"` — proving observable behaviour, not returns, drives the level, in the direction that matters. **Then re-verified live in the actual browser UI** on a fresh signup: locked card → primer → a deliberately wrong Q1 answer → real re-teach copy shown → corrected → all 3 correct → the REAL `unlockFeatureFn` server function fired (not the script's replicated logic) → Options page unlocked immediately, persisted across a full page reload, Margin stayed independently locked. 375px + desktop screenshots captured for the locked card, primer, quiz, wrong-answer re-teach, correct-answer state, and the unlocked page; the quiz dialog's mobile bottom-sheet layout (the same `SHEET_CONTENT_CLASS` already proven for `ConfirmDialog`/`OptionOrderPanel`) confirmed at 375px.

  - Commits: `lib/coaching/` pure modules + unit tests, migration `0024`, `types.ts` (3 files/commit chunks) → server function + client wrapper → `UnlockGate` + route wiring → `/app/coach` extension → this HANDOFF/PLAN update. **NOT pushed — reporting first per the kickoff's explicit instruction.**

- **2026-08-11 (PLAN.md §6 step 7 — B2 behavioural analytics — BUILT, VERIFIED live with real DB reconciliation, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Issue #7 moved to "In progress" on the board.**
  - **Intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** name the user's OWN patterns back to them, computed entirely from their real ledger — the input to step 8's adaptive coaching, so built as reusable, pure, testable functions rather than one-off UI math.
  - **`lib/behavioral/metrics.ts` — 6 patterns, all pure, all deterministic, zero AI calls.** Foundation is `reconstructStockClosingEvents`/`reconstructOptionClosingEvents`: this schema has no lot tracking (holdings/option_positions store one weighted-average cost per symbol), so "closed positions" are RECONSTRUCTED by replaying the transaction ledger chronologically, mirroring `execute_trade`/`execute_option_trade`'s own SQL math exactly (buys recompute a weighted avg; sells never touch it) — provably consistent with the real engine, not an approximation of it.
    1. **Disposition effect** — compares winners' vs. losers' average holding period + realized return (min 5 closes per bucket).
    2. **Over-trading** — buckets trades by ISO week, compares realized return in above-median-activity weeks vs. at-or-below (min 4 weeks of data, min 5 closes/bucket).
    3. **Concentration** — computed from COST BASIS (qty × avg_cost), not live market value, so it needs zero price data and is purely ledger-derived; explicitly labeled as cost-basis-based everywhere it's shown. Current share is always shown once any trade exists; the HISTORICAL "% of time over 25%" claim is separately gated behind 5 distinct portfolio-composition samples.
    4. **Revenge trading / loss-chasing** — average $ size of the next 3 ENTRY trades (buy/buy_to_open only — deliberately excludes exits, whose dollar totals are mechanically smaller after a loss for reasons that have nothing to do with behaviour) after a loss, vs. a baseline that ALSO excludes the post-loss window itself (min 5 losses, min 10 pooled post-loss entries). The baseline-exclusion was a real design fix made during unit testing — see below.
    5. **Win rate vs. risk-adjusted return** — win rate plus mean(return)/stdev(return) across closes (an approximation, explicitly documented as NOT a formal annualized Sharpe ratio). The point per the kickoff: a high win rate with a weak risk-adjusted figure is flagged as a warning, not praised (min 8 closes).
    6. **Journal correlation** — average return for closes linked (by transaction id) to a journal note vs. not, using ONLY set-membership against a caller-supplied noted-id set — never journal content (min 5 per bucket).
  - **Every metric returns a uniform `MetricResult<T>`** (`{available, n, data}` or `{available:false, n, minRequired, reason}`) — the statistical-honesty requirement made structural, not a UI convention that could drift.
  - **Deliberate architectural constraint respected: the server function never touches `journal_entries`.** `journal_entries` has NO `service_role` grant (a hard-won CTO privacy decision from step 6 — see that changelog entry). `lib/behavioral/functions.ts`'s `getBehavioralAnalyticsFn` fetches transactions/option_transactions server-side (service_role already has SELECT — 0017/0010) but takes `notedTransactionIds`/`notedOptionTransactionIds` as plain CLIENT-supplied input — `lib/behavioral/api.ts` fetches those via the user's OWN authenticated session (`getNotedTransactionIds()`, already id-only, never `body`/`title`) before calling the server function. Grepped explicitly to prove this: zero overlap between every Anthropic-calling file and everything importing `lib/behavioral/*`; zero references to `.body`/`.title` anywhere in `lib/behavioral/`.
  - **A real design improvement found DURING unit testing, not left as a known gap:** the first version of `computeRevengeTrading` used a baseline that included ALL trades (even the post-loss ones being measured). A synthetic test where post-loss trades were 75% of total activity showed the effect getting diluted toward invisible — a user who revenge-trades a lot would have had this metric under-detect it. Fixed by excluding post-loss-window trades from the baseline by index; the same synthetic case then reconciles to an exact 3.0× ratio (the planted value), and a genuinely flat-sizing ledger reconciles to exactly 1.0×.
  - **`/app/coach` — a new dedicated page**, not a Portfolio section (justification: six patterns each need real explanatory copy plus a below-threshold state, which would clutter Portfolio's already-dense layout; matches the precedent Margin and Journal set once they had enough content for their own "mode" of use; gives step 8 a natural home to build on). Sidebar + mobile nav entry (`Compass` icon, between Journal and Watchlist). Six cards via `lib/behavioral/copy.ts` (pure, observational tone per the kickoff's own rule — "You've sold winners after 4 days... held losers for 19," never "you're doing it wrong"); a **real copy bug caught live** (browser-verified, not just unit-tested): the shared signed-percent formatter was putting a "+" on plain magnitudes like "your largest position is +37% of your portfolio," which reads like a gain — fixed with a separate unsigned formatter for concentration's two lines only (returns keep the correct sign).
  - **VERIFIED, every claim with real evidence:**
    - **Unit tests (`verify-behavioral-analytics.ts`), synthetic ledgers, answer known by construction:** every one of the 6 patterns is PLANTED and proven detected, then a NEUTRAL or OPPOSITE ledger is proven NOT falsely flagged (disposition: winners actually held longer → `soldWinnersFaster=false`; over-trading: n/a case; concentration: a genuinely diversified round-robin ledger → historical over-threshold rate <10%, only the honest unavoidable startup transient; revenge: flat-sized trades → ratio exactly 1.0; win-rate: a consistent healthy record → warning correctly `false`). Plus a hand-computed sanity check on the closing-event reconstruction itself (multi-buy avg-cost, partial-then-full close, fresh-lineage-after-close) and options (`expired` correctly books a 100% loss, `sell_to_close` reconciles exactly). 46/46 assertions pass.
    - **Live seeded-account run (`verify-behavioral-live.ts`), real Supabase, real `execute_trade` RPCs, real live quotes across 12 symbols:** built a 36-transaction realistic ledger (6 winners, 6 losers, 12 post-loss re-entries), created 3 real journal notes via the user's own session, fetched the real noted-id set (confirmed exactly 3, id-only), fetched real transactions via admin exactly as the server function does, and reconciled `computeBehavioralAnalytics`'s output against the real rows: disposition winners/losers N and average returns matched the planted ±3% exactly; win rate matched 6-of-12 exactly; revenge trading correctly flagged `sizedUpAfterLoss=true`; over-trading HONESTLY reported unavailable (all real trades landed within the same few seconds — nowhere near the 4-week minimum, a genuine limitation of testing a real-timestamp ledger in one fast run, not a bug). **RLS/isolation**: user B's own 2-trade history reconciled independently, never mixed with A's 36. **Below-threshold honesty, proven on a real account**: every one of B's 6 metrics correctly withheld with the right `minRequired`/`reason` — including confirming concentration's OWN special-cased semantics (current-state always shown, only the historical claim gated).

- **2026-08-10/11 (PLAN.md §6 step 6 — B1 trade journal — BUILT, VERIFIED live with real DB reconciliation, committed locally) — NOT pushed. Issue #6 moved to "In progress" on the board.**
  - **Design intent, verbatim from the kickoff:** the highest-value thing isn't a notes field — it's capturing intent BEFORE the outcome is known, then later pairing that stated reasoning with the measured result. That pairing is also the raw material for step 7 (behavioural analytics).
  - **Data model — one `journal_entries` table, migration `0023_journal.sql`.** A note attaches to a `transaction_id` or `option_transaction_id` (at most one, DB CHECK-enforced), never to a holding/`option_position` — those rows are EPHEMERAL (deleted the instant a position fully closes) while transactions/option_transactions are the PERMANENT, immutable ledger. `symbol` is denormalized onto every row so trade-linked and standalone entries share one filterable column. Also extended `execute_trade`/`execute_option_trade` (same signatures, additive-only) to return the new `transaction_id`/`option_transaction_id`, so the client can link a trade-time note to the EXACT row a trade just created rather than re-querying "most recent transaction" (racy under concurrent trades).
  - **Deliberate privacy decision — `journal_entries` has NO `service_role` grant, and this must stay that way.** First pass gave it one (matching the precedent of `transactions`/`insights`/`analytics_events`, which needed it for real server-side jobs — prunes, batch processors). CTO rejected it: nothing server-side touches journal content, the app reads/writes exclusively through the user's own authenticated session (same pattern as `watchlist`), and journal entries are the most personal data in the product (private reflections — fear, mistakes, revenge trading). Withholding the grant means even our own server tooling and any future admin console structurally CANNOT read them — enforced by Postgres, not policy. **Explicit constraint on PLAN.md step 10 (super-admin console): it will NOT be able to display a user's journal entries.** Lifting that needs a real product decision + a privacy-policy update, not a quiet grant. `0023`'s own comment says this explicitly so a future session doesn't "fix" it. `verify-journal.ts` was rewritten to prove ground truth via the test users' own authenticated sessions, not `admin` — more faithful verification (proves what the app itself can see) as a direct consequence.
  - **Trade-time capture — optional, non-blocking, a light prompt AFTER success, not a gate.** `JournalEntryDialog` ("Why this trade?", pre-filled trade context, Skip always available) pops up right after `executeTrade`/`executeOptionTrade` resolves, not inside the money-moving `ConfirmDialog` itself — deliberately kept separate so declining costs zero extra friction on the actual trade.
  - **Outcome pairing (`lib/journal/outcome.ts`, pure function) — the payoff feature.** For a stock buy: if the symbol is still held, compares CURRENT live price to the linked transaction's own fill price (a price-return %, not a blended avg-cost P&L — matches the spec's own example, "$211 → currently +12%"); if the position has since fully closed, uses the invariant that the LAST transaction for that symbol must be the sell that zeroed it (no lot-tracking exists in this schema, so this is the honest, defensible reconstruction, not a precise per-lot number) and labels it "closed," not "current." Options mirror this using the enriched `getOptionPositions()` valuation (open) or the last closing `option_transactions` row — `sell_to_close`/`expired`/`settled` (closed).
  - **Surfaces:** `routes/app.journal.tsx` (nav entry between Options and Watchlist, sidebar + mobile) — chronological list, symbol/title/text search, an empty state that TEACHES ("writing down WHY you're making a trade, before you know how it turns out, is the only way to tell good reasoning from a lucky guess") rather than just saying nothing's here. A small notebook-icon indicator (deep-links to `/app/journal?symbol=X`) appears next to any transaction-history row with a linked note (`app.portfolio.tsx`). A new "Journal (N)" tab on `app.stock.$symbol.tsx` alongside Position/Options/News/About shows every entry tagged to that symbol, trade-linked or standalone, with a quick-add link. Edit/delete both reuse the existing `ConfirmDialog` for the destructive path.
  - **🛑 Real bug found + fixed during 0023's own verification (not discovered live):** the FIRST version of the migration granted `authenticated` but never `service_role` on `journal_entries` — the same grant-gap CLASS documented 3× already in this file (`transactions`, `insights`, `analytics_events`). Caught immediately by the verify script's own DB cross-check (`permission denied for table journal_entries`). Initially fixed with a grant migration matching the `0017` precedent — then CTO overruled that fix on privacy grounds (see above) and the grant was deliberately removed, not applied. The regression-suite risk this bug WOULD have posed if shipped: none, since no server job touches this table — but it would have broken any future admin/support tooling silently, exactly the failure mode the standing "grant/silent-write" rule exists to prevent.

    - **`verify-journal.ts` (new, 2 throwaway users, all real Supabase/RPCs, zero mocks):** real `execute_trade` buy → real `transaction_id` returned → trade-linked note created via the user's own RLS-scoped session → outcome reconciles EXACTLY to a fresh live quote. **The key property, proven explicitly:** bought MSFT, noted it, sold ALL of it (holdings row genuinely deleted) — the journal entry SURVIVES, outcome correctly flips to `status: "closed"` with `comparePrice` reconciling to the EXACT sell price that closed it. Same for a full option buy→note→sell_to_close cycle (premiums reconcile exactly, `changePct` to the cent). Standalone entry, edit (`updated_at` trigger fires), delete, RLS isolation both directions (user B can SELECT/UPDATE/DELETE zero rows of user A's notes; verified via A's own session, not admin), and both CHECK constraints (dual-link rejected, blank body rejected). **34/34 assertions passed.**

This can't be undone.") → empty state re-appears after delete, confirmed to teach rather than just say "nothing here." Screenshots at 375px and desktop.

Separately found every importer of `lib/journal/*` and confirmed none also imports an Anthropic-calling module (the one shared page, `app.stock.$symbol.tsx`, renders the AI Insight card and the Journal tab as independent features with no data flow between them). Extends the existing `handle_new_user()` trigger to REQUIRE `raw_user_meta_data ->> 'terms_accepted_version'` and `RAISE EXCEPTION` if absent, aborting the whole `auth.users` INSERT. **Corrected framing (CTO catch during review):** the migration's own comment originally overstated this as "unbypassable" — wrong, since `raw_user_meta_data` is client-supplied on `signUp()` and a scripted client can populate the field with any value it wants. What it actually guarantees is DATA INTEGRITY — no profile can exist without SOME consent record, so a bug in our own signup form can't silently skip it — not a security control. Comment rewritten to say this accurately, in both the migration and the matching call site in `auth.tsx`.

- **2026-08-10 (PLAN.md §6 step 5 — A2 abuse guards + A3 ops visibility — BUILT, VERIFIED live with real network delivery/DB proof at every seam, committed locally) — NOT pushed. A4 (legal surface) explicitly excluded from this step per instruction — drafts already exist in `legal/`, still need wiring.**
  - **Why this step, why now, verbatim from the kickoff:** the last technical gate before open public signup. Today anyone who signs up can burn paid Anthropic budget with zero limit, and if the app breaks for a stranger we'd never know.
  - **A2 — rate limiting.** Migration `0019_rate_limits.sql`: an append-only `rate_limit_events` table (chosen over a fixed-window counter specifically because a burst limit needs a TRUE rolling window — a fixed clock-minute bucket lets a user burst 2× at the boundary) plus an atomic `check_and_record_rate_limit()` plpgsql function, advisory-lock-guarded on `(user_id, action)` to close the same TOCTOU double-spend race every other money-moving function in this schema (`execute_trade`, `fund_agent`) is written to avoid — a naive "SELECT count in JS, then INSERT" has a real race two near-simultaneous requests can both slip through.
  - **Limits and reasoning** (`lib/rateLimit/check.server.ts`): **insight** — 10/5min burst, 50/day, checked ONLY on a genuine cache miss (a real Claude call), never on a free repeat view of an already-generated-today symbol — this precisely targets the actual cost vector (a burst across many DISTINCT symbols), matching the kickoff's own observation that per-stock insights are globally shared and near-zero marginal cost after the first hit. **agentRun** ("Run agent now") — 3/5min burst, 20/day, checked on EVERY invocation regardless of whether AI is enabled, since the quant scan alone is a real burst of provider requests. The daily brief and the cron's own agent-thinker run are cron-only (confirmed via grep — no client-callable server function reaches them) and deliberately unguarded; the watchdog makes no Claude calls and only re-prices the caller's own already-bounded holdings, also deliberately unguarded. Full reasoning lives in the migration's header comment.
  - **A3 — ops visibility.** Migration `0020_ops_visibility.sql`: `cron_heartbeats` (one row per job, upserted every run regardless of outcome — chosen over inferring freshness from business data specifically because an agent-thinker run with zero eligible agents that day would look identical to "never ran" if inferred from `agent_decisions` row counts) and `analytics_events` (first-party Postgres — my proposed choice over a third-party tool: no new external account needed to ship+verify today, data never leaves infra we already operate and are accountable for under our own Privacy Policy, genuinely free, and `track()` — `lib/analytics/track.server.ts` — is a thin enough abstraction that pointing it at PostHog/Plausible later is a one-file change). Sentry (`@sentry/react` client + `@sentry/node` server) wired at exactly two seams — the root error boundary and the two server-side catch-alls (`start.ts` middleware, `server.ts` fetch handler) — deliberately NOT into each of the ~20 individual server functions' own try/catch, since those mostly catch EXPECTED user-facing rejections ("insufficient buying power"), not bugs; routing every one of those into Sentry would be noise, not signal. `/api/health` (same CRON_SECRET auth as the other cron endpoints) checks DB reachability, the market-data pipeline (via the existing cache path — bounded extra cost, same TTL arithmetic as the rest of the app), and both daily crons' heartbeat freshness (26h staleness threshold — daily cadence plus drift buffer, not a tight SLA).
  - **Retention — closing a promise-vs-reality gap Venky flagged before verification:** legal/privacy.md commits "Error logs and analytics — retained for a limited period on a rolling basis and then discarded." `rate_limit_events` already pruned at 2 days (only the 24h daily window is ever read). Added `lib/analytics/prune.server.ts` — 90 days for `analytics_events` (long enough for real month-over-month product questions, genuinely limited rather than forever-in-practice), wired into the same daily cron chain as the other two prunes. Deletes unconditionally on age, which also ages out the ANONYMIZED (`user_id IS NULL`) rows a deleted account leaves behind — the retention promise holds for those too, not just still-linked rows.
  - **🐛 REAL BUG found during live verification, fixed + RE-VERIFIED FIXED:** triggering the real snapshot cron showed `analyticsPrune` failing with "permission denied for table analytics_events" — migration `0020` granted `service_role` only SELECT and INSERT, forgetting DELETE, so the prune had been silently failing every run since `0020` was applied (same class of gap as `0017`'s transactions/insights grants). Fixed with migration `0021_analytics_delete_grant.sql`, applied by Venky. **Re-verified live** (`verify-analytics-prune-fix.ts`): seeded one row backdated 100 days + one fresh row, ran the real `runAnalyticsPrune()` — completed with NO error (`{"deleted":1}`), the 100-day row confirmed gone, the fresh row confirmed still present. Also re-triggered the real `/api/cron/snapshot` HTTP endpoint end-to-end and confirmed `analyticsPrune` no longer errors (`{"deleted":0}` on the real, otherwise-empty table). The retention promise is now genuinely honoured, not just aspirational.

This is an intentional, documented divergence from every other table in this schema (which all CASCADE), not a residual-risk gap — matches how privacy-respecting analytics tools (Plausible, GA4) keep anonymous historical counts after an opt-out.

Event id `7b6edca8016f4990a298033e6472695e` for Venky to look up directly.

Then two DELIBERATE simulated-unhealthy proofs: backdated the snapshot heartbeat 3 days → 503 with `"Stale — last run older than the expected daily cadence."` for snapshot only (agent-thinker correctly unaffected), restored via a real cron re-trigger; temporarily pointed the DB check at a nonexistent table (source edit + revert, same pattern used earlier this session for the margin-monitor timeout investigation) → 503 with the real Postgres error message, reverted, confirmed back to 200. Unauthorized (missing/wrong secret) → 401 in both cases.

- **2026-08-10 (PLAN.md §6 step 3 — live prices + live intraday chart — BUILT, VERIFIED with real measured numbers + browser verification, committed locally) — NOT pushed.**
  - **The prime directive:** live updating must not meaningfully increase provider calls, since step 2's durable cache is what makes this affordable at all. **Headline number, measured not asserted:** 20 simulated concurrent users polling the SAME symbol for 2 real minutes cost **4 total provider calls** (not 20×8=160) — matches the arithmetic (`2 calls/min × 2min`) to the call. A second scenario — 5 distinct symbols × 4 users each, 20 total users, 2 real minutes — cost **16 provider calls**, scaling with SYMBOL COUNT not user count (≈4/symbol × 5 symbols; 4× the 1-symbol scenario's cost for the identical 20 users). Both measured via `verify-live-prices.ts` (new), instrumenting the real Finnhub fetch counter (`fetchStats()`) around real `getServerQuote()` calls on the actual L1+L2 cache path — no mocks.
  - **1. Poll-vs-TTL arithmetic, deliberately chosen and documented (not left implicit):** quotes poll every 15s client-side (`useQuotes.ts`'s new `QUOTE_POLL_MS`) against the existing 30s server TTL (`cache.server.ts`'s `TTL.quote`) ⇒ every other poll lands inside the still-fresh cache window, so a distinct symbol costs AT MOST 1 provider call per 30s (2/min) regardless of how many users/tabs/pages are watching it — they all read through the SAME L1+L2 cache from step 2. On the 1D range only, candles poll every 60s (`LivePriceChart.tsx`'s new `CANDLE_POLL_MS`) against the existing 5-min candle TTL ⇒ ≤1 provider call per 5 min per (symbol, 1D) combo; every other range (1W/1M/3M/1Y/ALL) never polls at all, unchanged from before this step.
  - **2. Two hard efficiency gates, both required, both built as one shared hook — `lib/marketData/useMarketLive.ts`:** `isOpen` (US market-hours, `isUsMarketOpen` — moved VERBATIM out of `lib/agent/cron.server.ts` into a new pure, client-safe `lib/marketData/marketHours.ts` with no server-only deps, specifically so the client polling gate and the server cron gate can never disagree about "is the market open"; `cron.server.ts` now imports + re-exports it for backward compat, confirmed via grep that nothing else imported it by the old path) rechecked every 30s client-side; `isTabVisible` via the Page Visibility API (`document.visibilityState`/`visibilitychange`), deliberately a custom listener rather than relying on any react-query implicit default. `isLive = isOpen && isTabVisible` feeds `refetchInterval: isLive ? POLL_MS : false` on both the quotes query and the 1D candles query — `false` is a hard stop, not a slowdown. **Both gates empirically verified live in the browser** (not just by code reading): with the REAL market closed (today is Sunday), zero new server-function calls over 40+ seconds of observation via `read_network_requests`/`performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`; then, with `isUsMarketOpen` TEMPORARILY forced to return `true` (reverted immediately after, confirmed via `git diff` showing zero residual change) to isolate the tab-visibility half specifically, toggling a simulated `document.visibilityState` hidden→visible→hidden showed polling stop within one interval of going hidden and resume within one interval of going visible, each transition confirmed via the network log. A `MarketStatusBadge` component ("Market closed — showing last close") surfaces the closed state on Dashboard/Portfolio/Markets/Watchlist/Stock Detail — verified rendering for real, since the market genuinely was closed throughout this verification.
  - **3. Tick-flash feedback, respects `prefers-reduced-motion`:** new `useTickFlash(value)` hook (diffs the previous vs. current value, ignoring the very first render so nothing flashes on initial paint) + `<FlashPrice>` wrapper component (a generic span-based reusable version for table price cells — Markets, Watchlist, Portfolio) and a dedicated `HoldingRow` component for Dashboard (extracted because React's Rules of Hooks forbid calling a hook inside a `.map()` callback directly — a deliberate two-pattern split: full-row extraction where the row markup was already complex, a light wrapper where only the price cell needed it). CSS keyframes in `styles.css` (`color-mix(in oklch, ...)` background flash, 700ms ease-out, up=gain-color/down=loss-color) wrapped in `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { animation: none; }` — disabled outright, not just shortened. Verified live: adding MSFT to a fresh watchlist showed a real green flash on first price load that faded within ~2s (confirmed via a follow-up screenshot), consistent with the 700ms animation, not a stuck state.
  - **4. Live 1D chart + scrub stability, the one item requiring genuine empirical (not just code-reading) proof:** `LivePriceChart.tsx`'s existing "swap the last bar for the live quote" trick only ever APPENDS new bars to the end of the series (never inserts/reorders), so an active scrub's `hoverIdx` (a plain array index) provably can't be invalidated by a live update — but this was verified in the browser, not just reasoned about: with the market temporarily forced open (same guarded, reverted technique as above), hovered a specific 1D bar (readout locked to "8/7/2026, 2:35:00 PM · Price: $311.93 · Selected: −$0.48 (−0.15%)"), then waited through 2+ full poll cycles (~60s) while 10 new `getQuotesFn`/`getCandlesFn` server calls fired in the background (confirmed via `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')`, count going from 4→14) — the readout was screenshotted again afterward and was BYTE-IDENTICAL, proving the scrub survived live data arriving underneath it.
  - **5. Consistency fix, closing a gap the task explicitly warned about:** Stock Detail's own-symbol quote previously lived in a SEPARATE react-query cache entry (`["quote", symbol]`) from every other page's shared `["quotes", ...]` key — meaning the exact scenario "a price shown in two places at once must not disagree" was structurally possible before this step. Unified onto one `useQuotes([symbol, ...otherSymbols])` call; the standalone query and its special-case holdings-value branch are gone. Now every price display for a given symbol, app-wide, reads the identical cache entry by construction.

Not a product bug; the underlying gate (`useMarketLive`) behaved exactly as coded once the harness state was corrected. Worth remembering for any future browser-driven visibility test in this tool.

- **2026-08-09 (PLAN.md §6 step 2 — durable Postgres price_cache — BUILT, VERIFIED live with real numbers, 3 pre-existing regressions found+fixed via a full regression re-run, committed locally in 4 chunks) — NOT pushed.**
  - **0. Consistency fix (asked to land first, closes a gap hit twice):** `0017_grants_consistency.sql` grants `service_role` SELECT on `public.transactions` (every sibling ledger table — `option_transactions`/`agent_transactions`/`margin_events` — already had this since the migration that created it; `transactions` was a lone `0002`-era oversight, not a security posture) and DELETE on `public.insights` (backlog item — insight rows had no prune path). Applied by Venky. **Definitively closed the one unproven claim from step 1's delete-account verification:** re-ran it with `transactions` promoted to a full 16th table read directly via `service_role` (previously only provable via the target user's own session, which structurally can't verify anything post-deletion) — `transactions: 1 → 0`, DB-proven, not inferred from the FK's `on delete cascade` clause.
  - **1. Two-tier cache, additive not destructive.** New `durableCached`/`durablePeekMany`/`durablePutMany` in `lib/marketData/cache.server.ts` sit ALONGSIDE the original Phase-5 `cached`/`cachePeek`/`cachePut` (untouched, still exactly what the options-chain cache and AI Insights' in-process fast path use — neither was asked to change, neither did). L1 = the same in-memory `Map` as before (dies every serverless invocation); L2 = the new `price_cache` table (`0018_price_cache.sql`, keyed by `(kind, symbol, interval)`, `jsonb` payload, `service_role`-only via RLS-with-zero-policies + explicit revokes, no client exposure). Read path L1→L2→provider, write-through on success only — `fn()` throwing propagates without touching either tier (verified: an error never "poisons" the cache with a bad payload). Wired into every ACTUAL provider-sourced call site: `getQuotesFn`/`getServerQuote` (quote), `getCandlesFn` (candles), `searchSymbolsFn` (search), `fhProfile`/`fh52Week`/`resolveSymbolName`/`fhMetrics`/`fhCompanyNews` (profile/metric/name/metricsfull/news — all in `finnhub.server.ts`), `getDailyHistory` (the heaviest single payload — 5Y of daily candles, shared by AI Insights' event study and the options vol estimator), and the What-If Simulator's own series fetch (`lib/simulator/functions.ts` — explicitly in scope: PUBLIC, the intended viral/high-traffic surface per PLAN.md §3, reading Twelve Data, the sharper-constrained provider). Every public signature (`getQuote`/`getQuotes`/`getCandles`/`searchSymbols`) unchanged — a pure infrastructure swap, confirmed by the full regression suite passing.
  - **2. Cache stampede — decided and documented, not left unconsidered (per instruction):** same-process concurrency already dedupes via the existing `inflight` Map (unchanged). Cross-process (two cold invocations racing before either's L2 write lands) is a real but BOUNDED race, accepted rather than building a Postgres advisory lock: the window is one provider round-trip (~100–500ms), worst case is a handful of duplicate calls not O(users), and a proper lock adds real complexity (timeout/stale-lock/retry handling) for a marginal win at today's real concurrency. Escalation path documented in the file's own header comment if usage ever shows sustained (not just this bounded) stampede.
  - **3. Retention:** new `lib/marketData/pruneCache.server.ts` (`runPriceCachePrune()`, 7-day threshold — the longest TTL in use is 24h, so 7 days is pure inspection headroom, not a freshness guarantee) folded into the EXISTING `/api/cron/snapshot` chain as a new final step, in its own try/catch exactly like every other step (expiry/interest/margin/snapshot), appended after snapshot since it's unrelated housekeeping — order relative to the money-critical steps doesn't matter and wasn't changed.

    - **HEADLINE, measured not asserted:** 20 simulated cold invocations (L1 explicitly cleared before each — the task's own sanctioned equivalent to a real separate process) requesting AAPL within one 30s TTL window → provider quote-fetch count went **0 → 1** across all 20, not 0 → 20. Exactly one `price_cache` row written (an upsert, not 20 duplicate inserts).
    - **TTL expiry:** backdated the cached row's `fetched_at` to 60s ago (>30s TTL) instead of sleeping real wall-clock time — a request afterward correctly re-fetched (delta +1) and the row's `fetched_at` updated to "now" (256ms old at check time).
    - **Error non-poisoning:** a `durableCached` call with a throwing `fn` propagated the error (not swallowed) and wrote NOTHING to `price_cache`; an immediate retry with a succeeding `fn` actually invoked it (proving L1 wasn't poisoned either) and returned the real value.
    - **Prune:** inserted one row backdated 10 days + one fresh row → `runPriceCachePrune()` deleted exactly the old one (`deleted: 1`), left the fresh one untouched.
    - **Grant sanity:** `service_role` confirmed able to read `price_cache` (the RLS-zero-policies + explicit grant combination works as designed).

A fresh $25k account correctly rejected the $150k buy as `insufficient_funds` (the buying-power math was NEVER wrong — the TEST'S assumption was). Fixed: `targetDollars = round2(p0.cash * 1.5)` (1.5× ACTUAL starting cash, matching the original test's own 150k/100k=1.5× ratio) and the equity assertion now compares against `p0.cash`, not a literal.
    - `verify-hardening-pass.ts` had the SAME class of bug in two places (`buy $150,000 of AAPL`, later `buy $30,000 more AAPL`) plus two assertions hardcoding `cash === 100000` post-reset. Fixed identically (1.5× actual current cash for both buys; reset assertions now import and compare against `STARTING_CASH` from `lib/mockData.ts` — the same constant the real app uses — instead of a literal, so this can't silently go stale again on a future capital-default change). Along the way, a many-symbol `runSnapshots({onlyUserId})` call (post-thinker-run, ~6-7 symbols each needing profile/52-week/name enrichment) needed its test timeout raised from 25s to 60s — NOT a hang (confirmed: completed in 28042ms wall-clock, and a later simpler-account snapshot in the same run completed in <1s) — a real, honest, measured cost of the L2 design on a COLD multi-symbol burst (each cold miss now does a Postgres round-trip before falling through to the provider), worth knowing about even though it's not a bug: it only affects first-ever-fetch-of-the-day per symbol, runs on an unattended daily cron not a live request, and is the direct trade-off for turning N provider calls into 1 — see residual risk below.
    - `verify-reset-account-live.ts` (the original C1b script) hardcoded `$100,000` in 4 assertions (RPC return value, `profiles.cash_balance`, the fresh post-reset snapshot, and a post-reset authenticated-session read) — all fixed the same way, importing `STARTING_CASH` instead of a literal `100000`.
    - **None of these were price-cache bugs** — confirmed by reading each failure's stack trace and the surrounding test setup before touching anything; every fix was to REPLACE a stale hardcoded assumption with either a proportional-to-actual-state calculation or a shared constant, never to weaken an assertion.

Residual risk / honest follow-up, not built here:** the bounded cold-cross-process stampede race (documented, not eliminated — see #2); the measured added latency on a COLD multi-symbol burst (a few seconds per NEW symbol needing 3 enrichment sub-fetches, each now doing an L2 round-trip before the provider call) — invisible for the STEADY-STATE case this feature targets (repeated reads of already-warmed symbols), real for the very first fetch of a brand-new symbol; `price_cache` rows currently only prune on the daily cron, so a burst of many distinct never-seen-again symbols (e.g. from search) would sit in the table for up to 7 days before cleanup — bounded, not unbounded, but worth knowing.

Simulated an old-style account (`starting_capital=100000`) and called `reset_paper_account`: returned `cash_balance:25000`, and post-reset `starting_capital` also read exactly `25000` — confirming reset lands on the CURRENT default, not the account's own original amount, exactly as designed.
